\medterm{Chronic Pelvic Pain} Persistent or recurrent pain in the lower abdomen or pelvis lasting at least six months.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Chronic Traumatic Encephalopathy} A progressive brain disorder associated with repeated head impacts and characterized by cognitive, mood, or behavioral changes.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cold Urticaria} Hives or swelling triggered by exposure of the skin to cold.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Colon Polyps} Growths arising from the inner lining of the colon; some types can develop into colorectal cancer.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Common Cold in Babies} A viral infection of an infant's upper respiratory tract that commonly causes a runny nose, congestion, and cough.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Compulsive Sexual Behavior} Recurrent sexual thoughts or behaviors that are difficult to control and cause significant distress or impairment.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Congenital Heart Defects in Children} Structural abnormalities of the heart or major blood vessels present at birth.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Congenital Heart Disease in Adults} A heart defect present from birth that persists into adulthood or causes complications later in life.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Congenital Mitral Valve Anomalies} Structural abnormalities of the mitral valve that are present at birth and may impair blood flow.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Congenital Myasthenic Syndromes} A group of inherited disorders that impair communication between nerves and muscles, causing fatigable weakness.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Constipation in Children} Infrequent, hard, or painful bowel movements in a child.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cough Headaches} Headaches triggered by coughing, sneezing, straining, or other sudden increases in pressure in the head.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cutaneous B-Cell Lymphoma} A type of non-Hodgkin lymphoma that primarily affects B lymphocytes in the skin.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cyclic Vomiting Syndrome} Recurrent episodes of severe vomiting separated by symptom-free intervals.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cyclosporiasis} Cyclosporiasis is a disease, symptom, syndrome, or clinical finding; diagnosis and management depend on its characteristic features, causes, severity, examination, and appropriate investigations.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cyclothymia} A long-term mood disorder with repeated periods of hypomanic and depressive symptoms that do not meet criteria for full episodes.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cholestasis of Pregnancy} A liver disorder of pregnancy in which impaired bile flow causes itching and elevated bile acids.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Choroid Plexus Carcinoma} A malignant tumor arising from the choroid plexus, the tissue that produces cerebrospinal fluid.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Chemo Brain} Subjective difficulties with memory, attention, or thinking that occur during or after cancer treatment.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Chest Tube} A flexible tube inserted into the pleural space to remove air, blood, or fluid and help the lung re-expand.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Culture} A laboratory test in which a clinical specimen is placed in conditions that allow microorganisms to grow and be identified; susceptibility testing may help guide treatment.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Calvaria} The dome-shaped upper portion of the skull that protects the brain.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Calyx} A cup-shaped structure that collects fluid or supports an organ. In the kidney, renal calyces collect urine from the renal pyramids and convey it to the renal pelvis.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cannula} A hollow tube inserted into a blood vessel, body cavity, or tissue to deliver or remove fluids or to provide access for instruments.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Canthotomy} A surgical incision through the outer canthus of the eyelids, usually performed urgently to relieve pressure in the orbit.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Canthus} The angle where the upper and lower eyelids meet.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Carbon Dioxide} A colorless gas produced by cellular metabolism and removed mainly by the lungs during exhalation.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cardia} The part of the stomach surrounding the opening where the esophagus joins the stomach.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cast} A rigid or semi-rigid device applied around an injured limb or joint to immobilize it while bone or soft tissue heals.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cauterization} The deliberate destruction or sealing of tissue using heat, electricity, chemicals, or another energy source to control bleeding or remove abnormal tissue.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Chlamydia trachomatis} A bacterium that commonly causes sexually transmitted infection of the urogenital tract and can also cause conjunctivitis or pneumonia.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Clamp} A surgical instrument used to grasp, compress, occlude, or hold tissue, blood vessels, tubing, or other structures.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Clean-Catch Specimen} A urine sample collected after cleansing the surrounding area and beginning urination, with the middle portion collected in a sterile container to reduce contamination.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Colposcope} A lighted, magnifying instrument used to examine the cervix, vagina, or vulva in detail.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Continuous Bladder Irrigation} Continuous instillation of sterile fluid through a urinary catheter to keep the bladder catheter patent and remove blood clots or debris.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Continuous Subcutaneous Insulin Infusion} Delivery of rapid-acting insulin through a pump and a subcutaneous infusion set to provide basal insulin and mealtime doses.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Convergence} The inward movement of both eyes so that they are directed toward the same nearby object.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Creatine} A nitrogen-containing compound stored mainly in skeletal muscle that helps supply energy for short, high-intensity activity.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cross-Matching} A laboratory test that checks donor and recipient blood for compatibility before transfusion.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cystoscope} A thin, lighted instrument used to examine the urethra and bladder and, when needed, perform procedures.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cystotome} A surgical instrument used to make an incision in the capsule of the lens during selected eye procedures.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cancer Grading} Assessment of how abnormal cancer cells appear under a microscope and how aggressively the tumor is likely to behave. It differs from cancer staging, which describes the extent of spread.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{C1 Esterase Inhibitor} A plasma protein that regulates the complement, kallikrein-kinin, and coagulation pathways. Deficiency or dysfunction can cause hereditary angioedema.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{C1q Nephropathy} A glomerular disease characterized by dominant or codominant deposits of the complement component C1q in glomeruli, with variable proteinuria and kidney dysfunction.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Café Coronary} Sudden airway obstruction by food, usually occurring while eating and sometimes causing collapse or death if the airway is not rapidly cleared.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Calcific Tendinitis} Deposition of calcium hydroxyapatite crystals within a tendon, most often the rotator cuff, causing acute pain and restricted movement.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Callosotomy} Surgical division of the corpus callosum to reduce the spread of certain severe, treatment-resistant seizures between the cerebral hemispheres.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Candidal Vaginitis} Inflammation of the vagina caused by overgrowth of Candida yeast, usually producing itching, soreness, and a thick white discharge.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Caffeine Intoxication} A toxic state caused by excessive caffeine exposure, characterized by symptoms such as restlessness, nervousness, insomnia, rapid heartbeat, tremor, gastrointestinal upset, or agitation.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Café-au-Lait Macules} Flat, light-brown skin patches with relatively uniform pigmentation. Multiple lesions may be associated with genetic disorders such as neurofibromatosis type 1.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cerebral Vasospasm} Abnormal narrowing of cerebral arteries that can reduce brain perfusion and cause delayed cerebral ischemia, especially after aneurysmal subarachnoid hemorrhage.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cervical Myelopathy} Dysfunction of the spinal cord caused by compression in the cervical spine, often producing hand clumsiness, gait disturbance, weakness, sensory changes, or abnormal reflexes.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cardiac Arrhythmias} Abnormalities of the heart rate or rhythm caused by disturbances in impulse formation or conduction. They include tachycardia, bradycardia, and irregular rhythms.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cerebral Thrombosis} Formation of a blood clot within a cerebral artery, reducing blood flow and potentially causing an ischemic stroke.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cervical Intraepithelial Neoplasia (CIN)} Abnormal precancerous changes in the squamous cells of the cervix, usually associated with persistent high-risk human papillomavirus infection. CIN is graded by the severity of dysplasia.



\textbf{Synonyms}:
CIN

\medterm{Chilblains (Pernio)} Itchy, painful, red or purplish inflammatory lesions caused by an abnormal response of small blood vessels to cold and subsequent rewarming.



\textbf{Synonyms}:
Pernio

\medterm{Cognition} The mental processes involved in acquiring, storing, retrieving, and using information, including attention, memory, language, reasoning, and judgment.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Compound Fracture (Open Fracture)} A fracture associated with a wound that communicates between the fracture site and the external environment. It carries increased risks of infection and tissue damage.



\textbf{Synonyms}:
Open Fracture

\medterm{Conversion Disorder (Functional Neurological Symptom Disorder)} A condition in which neurological symptoms, such as weakness, abnormal movements, sensory loss, or seizures, are not explained by a recognized structural neurological disease and cause significant impairment.



\textbf{Synonyms}:
Functional Neurological Symptom Disorder

\medterm{Corneal Graft (Corneal Transplant)} Surgical replacement of part or all of a diseased or damaged cornea with donor corneal tissue to restore vision or relieve pain.



\textbf{Synonyms}:
Corneal Transplant

\medterm{Coronary Care Unit (CCU)} A specialized hospital unit providing continuous monitoring and treatment for patients with serious acute cardiac conditions.



\textbf{Synonyms}:
CCU

\medterm{Crepitation (Crepitus)} A crackling, grating, or popping sensation or sound produced by movement of tissues, air, or bone. Causes include fractures, joint disease, subcutaneous air, and lung disease.


\textbf{Synonyms}:
Crepitus

\medterm{CABG} Coronary artery bypass grafting, a surgical procedure that creates a route around a narrowed or blocked coronary artery to improve blood flow to heart muscle.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Coronary artery bypass grafting; coronary bypass surgery.

\medterm{Cardiac} Relating to the heart.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Heart-related; cardiac-related.

\medterm{Cardiopulmonary} Relating to the heart and lungs or to their combined function.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Cardiorespiratory.

\medterm{Cardiopulmonary Bypass Machine} A machine that temporarily circulates and oxygenates blood during selected heart or major-vessel operations while the heart and lungs are not performing their usual functions.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Heart-lung machine; cardiopulmonary bypass pump.

\medterm{Cardiorespiratory Endurance} The ability of the heart, lungs, and circulation to deliver oxygen during sustained physical activity.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Cardiorespiratory fitness; aerobic endurance.

\medterm{Carminative} A substance traditionally used to reduce intestinal gas or discomfort by promoting passage of gas through the digestive tract.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Antiflatulent; gas-relieving agent.

\medterm{Carotenoids} Plant pigments, including beta-carotene, that contribute yellow, orange, or red color and may have antioxidant or provitamin-A activity.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Carotenoid pigments; provitamin-A pigments.

\medterm{Catagen} The transitional phase of the hair-growth cycle between active growth and resting.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Catagen phase; regression phase of hair growth.

\medterm{Catastrophic Reaction} A disproportionately intense emotional or behavioral response to a situation that may appear minor to others, often occurring with cognitive impairment or neurologic disease.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Catastrophic response; catastrophic emotional reaction.

\medterm{Cathartic} An agent that promotes bowel evacuation, usually by increasing stool water, intestinal motility, or both.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Purgative; laxative.

\medterm{Cation} An ion with a positive electrical charge, such as sodium, potassium, calcium, or hydrogen.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Positively charged ion.

\medterm{Cauda Equina} The bundle of lumbar, sacral, and coccygeal nerve roots below the end of the spinal cord.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Cauda equina nerve roots; horse-tail nerve bundle.

\medterm{Causalgia} Severe persistent burning pain and sensory disturbance caused by injury to a peripheral nerve, now commonly classified within complex regional pain syndrome type II.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Complex regional pain syndrome type II; causalgic pain.

\medterm{Cavity} A localized hole or structural defect in a tooth caused by dental caries.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Dental cavity; carious lesion.

\medterm{CBC} A blood test that measures red cells, white cells, hemoglobin, hematocrit, and platelets.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Complete blood count; full blood count.

\medterm{CCU} A hospital unit providing continuous monitoring and specialized treatment for people with serious acute cardiac disease.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Coronary care unit; cardiac care unit.

\medterm{Cell Senescence} A state in which a cell remains metabolically active but permanently stops dividing, often in response to aging, stress, or cellular damage.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Cellular senescence; replicative senescence.

\medterm{Central (Brain) Fatigue} A sense of reduced mental energy, concentration, alertness, or motivation arising primarily from central nervous-system processes.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Central fatigue; mental fatigue.

\medterm{Cerebrovascular} Relating to the blood vessels and circulation of the brain.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Brain-vascular; cerebral vascular.

\medterm{Cervicogenic Headache} A headache caused by a disorder of the cervical spine or its soft tissues, with pain referred from the neck to the head.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Cervical headache; neck-related headache.

\medterm{CFS} Chronic fatigue syndrome, a condition involving substantial persistent fatigue with post-exertional worsening and associated symptoms that are not explained by another disorder.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Chronic fatigue syndrome; myalgic encephalomyelitis/chronic fatigue syndrome.

\medterm{Challenge Testing} A controlled exposure to a suspected trigger, such as a food or medication, to assess whether it produces a reproducible reaction.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Provocation testing; challenge test.

\medterm{Chemical Peel} Application of a chemical solution to the skin to produce controlled exfoliation and regeneration of superficial or deeper skin layers.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Chemexfoliation; chemical resurfacing.

\medterm{Chemonucleolysis} An intradiscal treatment intended to dissolve part of a herniated intervertebral disk, historically using an enzyme preparation.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Enzymatic disk dissolution; chemonucleolysis procedure.

\medterm{Chiropractor} A healthcare practitioner who provides manual or manipulative treatment, especially for musculoskeletal conditions, according to the practitioner's training and local regulation.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Chiropractic practitioner; chiropractic clinician.

\medterm{Chlorophyllin} A water-soluble, copper-containing derivative of chlorophyll used in some products and studied for effects such as odor control; clinical uses depend on evidence and context.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Chlorophyllin copper complex; sodium copper chlorophyllin.

\medterm{Chlorosis} An outdated term for a greenish or pale complexion historically associated with severe iron-deficiency anemia, especially in young women.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Green sickness; hypochromic anemia, historical usage.

\medterm{Cholagogue} A substance that promotes emptying of the gallbladder into the intestine.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Gallbladder stimulant; biliary evacuant.

\medterm{Choleretic} A substance that promotes production or secretion of bile by the liver.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Bile-secretory agent; bile-flow stimulant.

\medterm{Cholesterol} A lipid required for cell membranes and synthesis of steroid hormones and bile acids. Excess atherogenic cholesterol can contribute to arterial plaque.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Serum cholesterol; blood cholesterol.

\medterm{Cholinergic Neuron} A nerve cell that releases acetylcholine as its principal neurotransmitter.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Acetylcholine-releasing neuron; cholinergic nerve cell.

\medterm{Chondrocyte} A mature cartilage cell that produces and maintains the cartilage extracellular matrix.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Cartilage cell.

\medterm{Chronic Paroxysmal Hemicrania} A primary headache disorder causing frequent, short-lasting, severe one-sided headaches with autonomic symptoms and a characteristic response to indomethacin.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
CPH; chronic paroxysmal hemicrania syndrome.

\medterm{Chyme} A semifluid mixture of partially digested food and gastric or intestinal secretions that passes from the stomach into the small intestine.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Digestive chyme; gastric chyme.

\medterm{Cicatricial Alopecia} Hair loss caused by destruction and scarring of hair follicles, usually leading to permanent loss in affected areas.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Scarring alopecia; cicatricial hair loss.

\medterm{CK} Creatine kinase, an enzyme found in skeletal muscle, heart muscle, and brain whose blood level may rise after tissue injury.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Creatine phosphokinase; creatine kinase enzyme.

\medterm{Clonal Expansion} Rapid multiplication of genetically or immunologically related cells derived from a single precursor after activation.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Clonal proliferation; clone expansion.

\medterm{Coagulate} To change from a liquid to a semisolid or solid state, especially the formation of a blood clot through coagulation.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Clot; clot formation.

\medterm{Cognitive Function} The mental processes involved in attention, memory, language, reasoning, learning, judgment, and problem solving.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Cognition; cognitive ability.

\medterm{Collagenase} An enzyme that breaks down collagen in connective tissue.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Collagen-digesting enzyme; collagen-degrading enzyme.

\medterm{Combined Hormone Therapy} Hormone therapy containing both estrogen and a progestogen, generally used when endometrial protection is needed in a person with a uterus.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Estrogen-progestogen therapy; combined menopausal hormone therapy.

\medterm{Combined Hyperlipidemia} A lipid disorder characterized by elevated cholesterol and triglycerides, often with increased atherogenic lipoproteins.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Mixed hyperlipidemia; combined hyperlipoproteinemia.

\medterm{Common Migraine} A migraine without a preceding aura.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Migraine without aura; migraine without neurologic aura.

\medterm{Compact Bone} Dense outer bone tissue organized into osteons that provides strength and structural support.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Cortical bone; dense bone.

\medterm{Complicated Migraine} An older, nonspecific term for migraine accompanied by prolonged or prominent neurologic symptoms; the specific syndrome should be identified when possible.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Migraine with prolonged neurologic symptoms; complex migraine, historical usage.

\medterm{Compounding Pharmacy} A pharmacy that prepares customized formulations for an individual patient when a suitable commercially manufactured product is unavailable or unsuitable.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Compounding pharmacy practice; extemporaneous pharmacy.

\medterm{Cones} Retinal photoreceptor cells responsible for color vision and detailed vision in relatively bright light.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Cone photoreceptors; cone cells.

\medterm{Conjugate Vaccine} A vaccine in which a poorly immunogenic polysaccharide antigen is linked to a carrier protein to improve the immune response, especially in infants and young children.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Protein-conjugate vaccine; conjugated polysaccharide vaccine.

\medterm{Conjugated Equine Estrogens} A mixture of estrogenic compounds historically derived from the urine of pregnant mares and used in some hormone-therapy formulations.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
CEE; conjugated estrogens.

\medterm{Continuous Combined Hormone Therapy} A hormone regimen in which estrogen and progestogen are taken continuously rather than sequentially.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Continuous combined HRT; continuous estrogen-progestogen therapy.

\medterm{Contractile Proteins} Proteins, especially actin and myosin, that interact to generate force and shorten muscle cells.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Muscle contractile proteins; actomyosin proteins.

\medterm{Contrast Medium} A substance administered or placed in the body to make tissues, organs, or body spaces more visible during imaging.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Contrast agent; radiographic contrast material.

\medterm{Controllers} Long-term asthma medicines taken regularly to reduce airway inflammation and prevent symptoms or exacerbations.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Controller medicines; maintenance asthma therapy.

\medterm{Convulsion} A sudden episode of involuntary, often rhythmic muscle contractions that may occur with a seizure or other disturbance of brain function.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Convulsive episode; seizure-related motor activity.

\medterm{Corn} A localized thickening of skin caused by repeated pressure or friction, often occurring on the toes or feet.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Clavus; heloma.

\medterm{Coronary} Relating to the arteries or circulation that supply the heart muscle.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Cardiac blood-vessel-related; heart-coronary.

\medterm{Coronary Artery Bypass Surgery} Surgery that uses a graft to route blood around a narrowed or blocked coronary artery.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
CABG; coronary bypass surgery.

\medterm{Coronary Endarterectomy} Surgical removal of atherosclerotic plaque from the inner lining of a coronary artery.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Coronary plaque endarterectomy; coronary endarterectomy procedure.

\medterm{Coronary Spasm} Temporary contraction of a coronary artery that reduces blood flow to the heart muscle and may cause angina or infarction.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Vasospastic angina mechanism; coronary artery spasm.

\medterm{Coronary Care Unit} A hospital unit providing continuous monitoring and specialized care for acute cardiac illness.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
CCU; cardiac care unit.

\medterm{Corpus Cavernosum} One of two columns of erectile tissue in the penis that fill with blood during erection.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Cavernous body; penile corpus cavernosum.

\medterm{Corpus Luteum} The temporary endocrine structure formed from an ovarian follicle after ovulation; it produces progesterone during the luteal phase and early pregnancy.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Yellow body; luteal body.

\medterm{Corpus Spongiosum} A column of erectile tissue surrounding the penile urethra and expanding distally to form the glans.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Spongy body; corpus spongiosum penis.

\medterm{Corrugator Muscle} A small facial muscle that draws the eyebrows downward and inward, contributing to frowning.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Corrugator supercilii; glabellar muscle.

\medterm{Cortex} The outer layer of an organ or structure, such as the cerebral cortex, adrenal cortex, or outer layer of a hair shaft.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Cortical layer; outer cortex.

\medterm{Cortical Bone} Dense bone forming the outer shell of most bones and providing much of the skeleton's mechanical strength.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Compact bone; cortical tissue.

\medterm{Corticotropin-Releasing Factor} A hypothalamic hormone that stimulates the pituitary gland to release adrenocorticotropic hormone and helps coordinate the stress response.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Corticotropin-releasing hormone; CRH.

\medterm{COX-2 Inhibitors} Medicines that inhibit cyclooxygenase-2, an enzyme involved in prostaglandin production and inflammation.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Cyclooxygenase-2 inhibitors; selective COX-2 inhibitors.

\medterm{CPR} CPR is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Cardiopulmonary resuscitation; basic life support measure.

\medterm{Craving} A strong, often difficult-to-control desire for a substance, behavior, or experience, commonly seen in substance-use disorders.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Strong urge; compulsive desire.

\medterm{Creatinine Test} A blood or urine test measuring creatinine to help assess kidney filtration and muscle-related creatinine production.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Creatinine measurement; renal function creatinine test.

\medterm{Crepitus} A crackling, grating, or popping sensation or sound caused by movement of tissue, bone, joint surfaces, or air in soft tissue.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Crepitation; crackling sensation.

\medterm{Crohn's Disease} A chronic inflammatory bowel disease that can affect any part of the gastrointestinal tract and commonly causes abdominal pain, diarrhea, weight loss, or systemic inflammation.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Crohn disease; regional enteritis.

\medterm{Crown} The visible part of a tooth above the gum line, or a dental restoration that covers and protects a damaged tooth.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Dental crown; anatomic tooth crown.

\medterm{CT} Computed tomography, an imaging method that uses multiple X-ray measurements and computer reconstruction to produce cross-sectional images.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Computed tomography; CAT scan.

\medterm{Cupping} An abnormal enlargement or depression of the optic-disc cup, often assessed in evaluating glaucoma.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Optic-disc cupping; glaucomatous cupping.

\medterm{Cushing's Syndrome} A disorder caused by prolonged exposure to excessive cortisol, producing features such as central weight gain, hypertension, skin changes, and muscle weakness.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Hypercortisolism; cortisol excess syndrome.

\medterm{Cuticle} The thin outermost layer of a hair shaft formed by overlapping cells that protect the inner cortex.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Hair cuticle; cuticular layer.

\medterm{Cyclic Guanosine Monophosphate} A cellular signaling molecule involved in smooth-muscle relaxation, phototransduction, and other physiologic processes.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
cGMP; cyclic GMP.

\medterm{Cyclic Hormone Therapy} Hormone therapy in which estrogen is given continuously or for part of a cycle and progestogen is added for a defined number of days.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Sequential hormone therapy; cyclic estrogen-progestogen therapy.

\medterm{Cyclooxygenase} An enzyme family that helps convert arachidonic acid into prostaglandins and related mediators involved in pain, inflammation, and platelet function.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
COX enzyme; prostaglandin-endoperoxide synthase.

\medterm{Cytokines} Small signaling proteins released by immune and other cells that regulate inflammation, immunity, blood-cell production, and tissue responses.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Immune signaling proteins; cytokine mediators.

\medterm{Cytotoxic Alopecia} Hair loss caused by exposure to a cytotoxic treatment or other toxic injury to actively growing hair follicles.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
Drug-induced alopecia; chemotherapy-related hair loss.
\medterm{C fiber} C fiber concerns c fiber, a biologic or physiologic concept used in research, laboratory medicine, and the explanation of disease.

\medterm{Hodgkin lymphoma (Hodgkin disease)} A cancer of the lymphatic system characterized by abnormal lymphocytes and often presenting with painless lymph-node enlargement, fever, sweats, or weight loss.
\textbf{Synonyms}:
\medterm{Hurthle cell cancer} A rare thyroid cancer arising from Hurthle cells, with behavior ranging from indolent to aggressive.
\textbf{Synonyms}:
\medterm{Mouth cancer} Cancer arising in the lips, gums, tongue, cheeks, floor of the mouth, palate, or other oral tissues.
\textbf{Synonyms}:
\medterm{Neuroendocrine tumors} Tumors arising from cells that receive signals from the nervous system and produce hormones; they may be benign or malignant.
\textbf{Synonyms}:
\medterm{Paraneoplastic syndromes of the nervous system} Neurologic disorders caused by an immune response to cancer rather than by direct tumor invasion or metastasis.
\textbf{Synonyms}:
\medterm{Squamous cell carcinoma of the skin} A skin cancer arising from squamous cells, often appearing as a scaly, crusted, wart-like, or ulcerated growth.

\medterm{Oral thrush} A fungal infection of the mouth, usually caused by Candida, producing creamy white patches, soreness, or altered taste.
\textbf{Synonyms}:
\medterm{Yeast infection (vaginal)} An overgrowth of Candida in the vagina causing itching, irritation, redness, and thick white discharge.
\textbf{Synonyms}:
\medterm{Sudden cardiac arrest} An abrupt loss of effective heart activity, causing unresponsiveness and absent or abnormal breathing; it is a medical emergency.
\textbf{Synonyms}:
\medterm{Myocardial ischemia} Reduced blood flow and oxygen supply to heart muscle, usually from narrowed coronary arteries, potentially causing chest pain or heart damage.
\textbf{Synonyms}:
\medterm{Enlarged heart} An increase in heart size caused by conditions such as high blood pressure, valve disease, anemia, or cardiomyopathy.

\medterm{Childhood apraxia of speech} A speech-sound disorder in which a child has difficulty planning and coordinating the movements needed for clear speech.

\medterm{Cataracts} Clouding of the eye's natural lens that causes blurred vision, glare, faded colors, or difficulty seeing at night.
\textbf{Synonyms}:

\textbf{Synonyms}:
\medterm{Median arcuate ligament syndrome (MALS)} Compression of the celiac artery and nearby nerves by the median arcuate ligament, potentially causing post-meal abdominal pain and weight loss.

\medterm{Cellulite} Cellulite concerns cellulite, a body structure, substance, or physiologic concept whose clinical meaning depends on location, function, and patient context.

\textbf{Synonyms}:
\medterm{Drug addiction (substance use disorder)} A chronic disorder involving compulsive substance use despite harmful consequences, with impaired control, cravings, tolerance, or withdrawal.
\textbf{Synonyms}:
\medterm{Chilblains} Painful, itchy, swollen skin lesions caused by an abnormal response to cold and damp conditions.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Depression (major depressive disorder)} A mood disorder causing persistent depressed mood or loss of interest along with cognitive, physical, and functional symptoms.

\medterm{Calcific Bursitis} Calcific bursitis occurs when calcium deposits form in or around a bursa, often near the shoulder, causing pain and restricted movement. Treatment may include activity modification, anti-inflammatory care, physical therapy, aspiration, injection, or removal of deposits.

\medterm{Canalith Repositioning Maneuvers} Canalith repositioning maneuvers move displaced inner-ear crystals from a semicircular canal back to the utricle. They are used for benign paroxysmal positional vertigo, with the maneuver selected according to the affected canal and side.

\medterm{Cancer Causes} Cancer develops when genetic and epigenetic changes allow cells to grow, survive, and invade abnormally. \medterm{Causes Symptoms of Hormonal Imbalances Women} Hormonal imbalance in women can reflect thyroid disease, polycystic ovary syndrome, menopause, pregnancy, adrenal or pituitary disorders, medicines, or changes in body weight. Symptoms may include irregular bleeding, acne, hair changes, infertility, hot flashes, fatigue, or mood changes.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cavities} A cavity is tooth decay caused by acids produced when oral bacteria metabolize dietary carbohydrates. The process can progress from enamel demineralization to dentin and pulp infection; fluoride, oral hygiene, diet, dental sealants, fillings, or root-canal treatment may be needed.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Central Precocious Puberty} Central precocious puberty begins when the hypothalamus and pituitary activate the normal puberty pathway unusually early. \medterm{cerebrovascular accident (Stroke Symptoms and Treatment)} Alternate index label for Stroke Symptoms and Treatment.

\medterm{Cervical Intraepithelial Neoplasia Cervical (Cervical Dysplasia)} Alternate index label for Cervical Dysplasia.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cervical Pain (Neck Pain)} Alternate index label for Neck Pain.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{CFS (Chronic Fatigue Syndrome)} Alternate index label for Chronic Fatigue Syndrome.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{chemotherapy treatment} Chemotherapy uses medicines that damage or inhibit rapidly dividing cancer cells. It may be given before or after local treatment, for advanced disease, or for symptom control; effects and monitoring depend on the drugs, cancer, treatment goal, and organ function.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Chest Pain on the Left Side Above a Female Breast} Left-sided chest or breast-area pain can arise from muscle or rib injury, breast tissue, reflux, lung disease, anxiety, or heart disease. Pressure, exertional pain, shortness of breath, sweating, faintness, or pain spreading to the arm or jaw requires emergency assessment.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{CHF (Congestive Heart Failure (CHF) Overview)} Alternate index label for Congestive Heart Failure (CHF) Overview.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Chickenpox (Varicella)} Alternate index label for Varicella.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Chickenpox Contagious (Is Chickenpox Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is Chickenpox Contagious.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Chiggers (Bites)} Alternate index label for Bites.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Chikungunya Virus Infection} Chikungunya is a mosquito-borne viral infection that usually causes abrupt fever and severe joint pain, sometimes with rash, headache, or conjunctivitis. Joint symptoms can persist; care is supportive, mosquito exposure should be reduced, and severe illness requires assessment.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Child Abuse Facts} Child abuse includes physical or emotional harm, sexual abuse, exploitation, or neglect by a caregiver or other person. Possible signs include unexplained injuries, developmental change, fear, poor hygiene, or inconsistent explanations; suspected abuse should be reported through local protection services or emergency channels.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Children Autism (Autism and Communication)} Alternate index label for Autism and Communication.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Children Dementia (Dementia)} Alternate index label for Dementia.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Children Night Sweats (Night Sweats)} Alternate index label for Night Sweats.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Children Seizures (Febrile Seizures)} Alternate index label for Febrile Seizures.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Children Separation Anxiety (Separation Anxiety)} Alternate index label for Separation Anxiety.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Children Sleep (Sleep and Sleep Disorders in Children and Teens)} Alternate index label for Sleep and Sleep Disorders in Children and Teens.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Children's Cough Causes and Treatments} Children cough with viral infection, asthma, allergies, reflux, airway irritation, or an inhaled object. Treatment depends on cause and age; breathing difficulty, stridor, blue color, dehydration, sudden onset, or a cough lasting beyond expected recovery needs medical evaluation.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Children's Health} Children's health includes growth, development, nutrition, immunization, sleep, mental health, injury prevention, and management of acute or chronic illness. Care is guided by developmental stage, family context, preventive visits, and early recognition of concerning changes.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Childrens Health (Children's Health)} Alternate index label for Children's Health.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{chlamydia bacteria infection (Chlamydia in Women Overview)} Alternate index label for Chlamydia in Women Overview.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Chlamydia in Women Overview} Chlamydia is a sexually transmitted infection caused by Chlamydia trachomatis and may cause no symptoms. Untreated infection can lead to cervicitis, pelvic inflammatory disease, infertility, ectopic pregnancy, or infection in a newborn; testing and treatment of partners are important.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{chloasma (Melasma)} Alternate index label for Melasma.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cholangiocarcinoma (Bile Duct Cancer (Cholangiocarcinoma))} Alternate index label for Bile Duct Cancer (Cholangiocarcinoma).

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Choledochal Cysts} Choledochal cysts are congenital or acquired dilations of the bile ducts that can cause abdominal pain, jaundice, pancreatitis, cholangitis, or stones. Imaging establishes the anatomy, and surgical removal is often recommended because of recurrent complications and cancer risk.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{cholelithiasis (Gallstones)} Alternate index label for Gallstones.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cholera Contagious (Is Cholera Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is Cholera Contagious.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cholesterol lowering (Cholesterol Management)} Alternate index label for Cholesterol Management.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cholesterol Management} Cholesterol management combines cardiovascular-risk assessment with dietary pattern, physical activity, tobacco avoidance, weight care, and medicines when indicated. Statins and other agents lower atherogenic lipoproteins, with intensity chosen according to prior cardiovascular disease and overall risk.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{chorioretinitis (Uveitis)} Alternate index label for Uveitis.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Chorioretinitis Toxoplasma (Toxoplasmosis)} Alternate index label for Toxoplasmosis.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Chronic Inflammatory Response Syndrome (CIRS)} Alternate index label for CIRS.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Churg Strauss Syndrome (Churg-Strauss Syndrome)} Alternate index label for Churg-Strauss Syndrome.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Ciguatera Poisoning} Ciguatera poisoning follows eating reef fish containing ciguatoxins. Gastrointestinal symptoms may be followed by neurologic sensations such as temperature reversal, tingling, itching, weakness, or abnormal heart rate; treatment is supportive and prevention depends on avoiding high-risk fish.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cirrhosis Primary Biliary (Primary Biliary Cirrhosis (PBC))} Alternate index label for Primary Biliary Cirrhosis (PBC).

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cleft Palate and Cleft Lip} Cleft lip and cleft palate are congenital openings caused by incomplete fusion of facial structures during development. They can affect feeding, speech, hearing, teeth, and breathing and are managed by coordinated surgical, dental, speech, hearing, and nutritional care.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Clinical depression (Depression)} Alternate index label for Depression.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Clostridium Difficile Colitis} Clostridioides difficile colitis is antibiotic-associated inflammation of the colon caused by toxin-producing bacteria. It causes watery diarrhea, abdominal pain, fever, and sometimes severe colitis; diagnosis and treatment depend on symptoms, testing, recurrence, and severity.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Clot Blood (Blood Clots)} Alternate index label for Blood Clots.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Club Drugs List and Side Effects} Club drugs include substances such as MDMA, ketamine, GHB, and others used recreationally in social settings. Effects may include agitation, hallucinations, seizures, dangerous temperature elevation, respiratory depression, cardiovascular toxicity, impaired consent, and overdose.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cluster Headaches} Cluster headache causes repeated attacks of severe one-sided orbital or temporal pain with tearing, nasal symptoms, eyelid drooping, or restlessness. Attacks occur in bouts and require targeted acute and preventive treatment; a first sudden severe headache needs urgent evaluation.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{CMV (Cytomegalovirus (CMV))} Alternate index label for Cytomegalovirus (CMV).

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Coats Disease (Coats' Disease)} Alternate index label for Coats' Disease.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cocaine and Crack Abuse} Cocaine and crack use can cause dependence, agitation, paranoia, seizures, stroke, heart attack, dangerous arrhythmias, pregnancy complications, and nasal or lung injury. Treatment combines safety assessment, management of intoxication or withdrawal, behavioral care, and support for substance-use disorder.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cold (Common Cold)} Alternate index label for Common Cold.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cold and Cough Medicine for Infants and Children} Many nonprescription cold and cough medicines are unsafe or ineffective for young children and can cause sedation, agitation, abnormal heart rate, overdose, or breathing problems. Age-appropriate fluids, saline, humidification, and clinician-guided treatment are safer approaches.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cold Flu Allergy Treatments (Cold, Flu, Allergy)} Alternate index label for Cold, Flu, Allergy.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cold Sore vs. Pimple (Pimple vs Cold Sore)} Alternate index label for Pimple vs Cold Sore.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Cold, Flu, Allergy} Colds, influenza, and allergies can all cause nasal symptoms and cough but differ in cause, fever pattern, duration, and contagiousness. Viral illness usually improves with supportive care, while allergies respond to trigger reduction and appropriate antihistamine or nasal treatment.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Colitis Collagenous (Lymphocytic Colitis)} Alternate index label for Lymphocytic Colitis.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Colitis Contagious (Is Colitis Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is Colitis Contagious.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Colitis Lymphocytic (Lymphocytic Colitis)} Alternate index label for Lymphocytic Colitis.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Colitis Microscopic (Lymphocytic Colitis)} Alternate index label for Lymphocytic Colitis.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Colitis Ulcerative (Ulcerative Colitis)} Alternate index label for Ulcerative Colitis.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Colon Cancer Prevention} Colorectal-cancer risk can be reduced through recommended screening, physical activity, healthy weight, limiting alcohol, avoiding tobacco, and a diet rich in fiber-containing plant foods. Screening can detect precancerous polyps and early cancer before symptoms develop.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{colonic diverticulitis (Diverticulosis)} Alternate index label for Diverticulosis.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{colorectal cancer (Colon Cancer)} Alternate index label for Colon Cancer.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Coma Diabetic (Diabetic Ketoacidosis)} Alternate index label for Diabetic Ketoacidosis.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Coma Myxedema (Myxedema Coma)} Alternate index label for Myxedema Coma.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Common Cold Stages and Timeline of Symptoms} The common cold often begins with sore throat, sneezing, or fatigue, followed by nasal congestion, runny nose, and cough. Symptoms usually peak within several days and improve over one to two weeks, although cough and fatigue may last longer.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Common Early Symptoms of Myasthenia Gravis} Early myasthenia gravis commonly causes fluctuating, fatigable weakness of the eyelids, eye movements, facial muscles, chewing, swallowing, speech, neck, or limbs. Symptoms worsen with use and improve with rest; breathing or swallowing difficulty is an emergency.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Common Medical Abbreviations and Terms} Medical abbreviations and terms summarize diagnoses, tests, medicines, anatomy, and treatment instructions. Because abbreviations can be ambiguous or unsafe, patients should ask clinicians or pharmacists to explain unfamiliar wording rather than infer its meaning.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Compulsive Obsessive Disorder (Obsessive Compulsive Disorder (OCD))} Alternate index label for Obsessive Compulsive Disorder (OCD).

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Conditions Caused by Deletion Mutations} Deletion mutations remove DNA segments and can disrupt one gene or several neighboring genes. The clinical effect depends on size, location, inheritance, and whether the remaining copy compensates; genetic counseling and testing may clarify diagnosis and recurrence risk.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{congenital megacolon (Hirschsprung Disease)} Alternate index label for Hirschsprung Disease.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Congestive Heart Failure (CHF) Overview} Alternate index label for CHF) Overvie.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Conjunctivitis (Pink Eye)} Alternate index label for Pink Eye.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Constipation (Laxatives For Constipation)} Alternate index label for Laxatives For Constipation.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Constipation Foods to Avoid (Top Foods That Cause Constipation)} Alternate index label for Top Foods That Cause Constipation.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Constipation Home Remedies (Constipation)} Alternate index label for Constipation.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{consumption (Tuberculosis (TB) Facts)} Alternate index label for Tuberculosis (TB) Facts.

\medterm{Contagious C. Diff Clostridium Difficile (Is C diff Clostridium difficile Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is C diff Clostridium difficile Contagious.

\medterm{Contagious Canker Sores (Are Canker Sores Contagious)} Alternate index label for Are Canker Sores Contagious.

\medterm{Contagious Cough (Is a Cough Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is a Cough Contagious.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Contagious Diverticulitis (Is Diverticulitis Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is Diverticulitis Contagious.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Contagious E. coli (Is E coli Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is E coli Contagious.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Contagious H. pylori (Is H pylori contagious)} Alternate index label for Is H pylori contagious.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Contagious Hepatitis (Is Hepatitis Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is Hepatitis Contagious.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Contagious Hepatitis A (Is Hepatitis A Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is Hepatitis A Contagious.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Contagious Hepatitis B (Is Hepatitis B Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is Hepatitis B Contagious.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Contagious Hepatitis C (Is Hepatitis C Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is Hepatitis C Contagious.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Contagious Sinus Infection (Is a Sinus Infection Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is a Sinus Infection Contagious.

\textbf{Synonyms}:

\medterm{Contagious Sore Throat (Is Sore Throat Pharyngitis Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is Sore Throat Pharyngitis Contagious.

\medterm{Contagious Strep Throat (Is Strep Throat Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is Strep Throat Contagious.
